# Project Agent Rules

## Operating Rule
- Read `AGENTS.md` before project edits.
- Update `memory/CHANGELOG.md` after meaningful code, config, or architecture changes.
- Update `memory/PROMPTS.md` with user prompts that define requirements.
- Store test outputs, logs, and verification notes under `test_results/`.
- Prefer agent-centered code: explicit contracts, deterministic tests, small files, low hidden state.

## Architecture Source
- Primary architecture contract: `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md`.
- Current Stream 4 implementation rule: use threshold + compact + CUB/fixed-temp sort/reduce + compact; do not use Stream 4 hash-table dedup.

## Implementation Guardrails
- `State128` logical bytes are `v[0..119]`.
- `State128` padding bytes are `v[120..127]`.
- Persistent frontier padding must be zero.
- `FinalResponse` may temporarily store `target_local_idx` in `v[120..123]`.
- `Hash128` is one logical 128-bit key stored as `{lo, hi}`.
- `CandidateMeta` must remain 32 bytes and 32-byte aligned.
- Stream 3 payload id must be original candidate id, not compact index.
- Stream 4 must not apply shard top-k or semantic shard cap.

For project repositories, read local project guidance before changing notebooks or source:

- `AGENTS.md`
- `memory/PROMPTS.md` and `memory/CHANGELOG.md`, especially recent Kaggle entries
- relevant `test_results/kaggle_*` reports for the same failure/config family

This project has many Kaggle-specific decisions preserved in memory. Do not assume a generic Kaggle workflow is correct when project memory says otherwise.

## Safety And Scope

- Do not upload or publish current uncommitted source to a Kaggle dataset unless the user explicitly approves external export.
- Prefer GitHub-backed Kaggle source when the user has selected GitHub as source of truth.
- Do not push a public Kaggle notebook/kernel unless the user explicitly approves publication or public push.
- Do not make C++/CUDA algorithmic changes while diagnosing Kaggle failures unless the user explicitly approves code changes. Config-only notebook changes are acceptable when useful and consistent with the request.
- Do not revert unrelated local changes. Kaggle notebook edits often coexist with dirty source changes.
- For multi-rank validation, do not fake `WORLD_SIZE=2` on one <truncated omitted_approx_tokens="707" />ed logs. This rule came from project memory and applies to diagnostic/watch tasks.
- Do not stop production/no-timeout runs unless the user explicitly asks or the notebook itself has a configured timeout.
- If a run appears hung because stdout is redirected, check rank log files and whether live-rank streaming is enabled before assuming a stall.
- Preserve logs even when stopping: terminate gracefully when possible, then download output files.

## Failure Diagnosis Checklist

After a Kaggle failure, collect and scan:

- Kaggle worker status and kernel version
- notebook stdout tail
- rank logs under the notebook output directory
- result JSONL/CSV summaries
- GPU budget/preflight output
- explicit Kaggle messages such as disk quota failures

Common patterns in this project:

- `KernelWorkerStatus.ERROR` plus "more disk space than is available" usually means disk quota/static history arena pressure.
- `return_code=-9` can indicate host RAM OOM.
- `code=3002` / `STREAM_FATAL_STREAM3_DOUBLE_BUFFER_OVERFLOW` points to Stream3/Stream4/threshold/capacity flow diagnostics.
- `final selected count does not match score phase counts` points to final selection/histogram/exact-count mismatch.
- `cudaGraphInstantiate: out of memory` may require config-only headroom/shard-capacity changes before deeper code work.
- Silent output after build may simply mean rank stdout is redirected and live rank logging is disabled.

Write a short report under `test_results/` for meaningful Kaggle validations, sweeps, failures, or launches.

## Response Style

- Be explicit about whether you changed source code, notebook config only, or only launched/monitored a run.
- Include Kaggle kernel slug/version/status when known.
- Include exact local report/log paths in `test_results/`.
- Keep recommendations tied to evidence from logs, not guesses.
- If the next step requires exporting source or publishing publicly, ask for explicit approval first.

# Project Agent Rules

## Operating Rule
- Read `AGENTS.md` before project edits.
- Update `memory/CHANGELOG.md` after meaningful code, config, or architecture changes.
- Update `memory/PROMPTS.md` with user prompts that define requirements.
- Store test outputs, logs, and verification notes under `test_results/`.
- Prefer agent-centered code: explicit contracts, deterministic tests, small files, low hidden state.

## Architecture Source
- Primary architecture contract: `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md`.
- Current Stream 4 implementation rule: use threshold + compact + CUB/fixed-temp sort/reduce + compact; do not use Stream 4 hash-table dedup.

## Implementation Guardrails
- `State128` logical bytes are `v[0..119]`.
- `State128` padding bytes are `v[120..127]`.
- Persistent frontier padding must be zero.
- `FinalResponse` may temporarily store `target_local_idx` in `v[120..123]`.
- `Hash128` is one logical 128-bit key stored as `{lo, hi}`.
- `CandidateMeta` must remain 32 bytes and 32-byte aligned.
- Stream 3 payload id must be original candidate id, not compact index.
- Stream 4 must not apply shard top-k or semantic shard cap.

# Stream1 Transformer Kaggle 2xT4 Benchmark v4 - 2026-07-01

Kernel: `trydotatwo/cayley-beam-transformer-2xt4-benchmark`
Version: 4
Status: `KernelWorkerStatus.COMPLETE`
Branch commit tested: `08d1ddd` (`Rewrite Stream1 transformer attention with tensor GEMM`)

## Best Rows

| GPU | B_MICRO | concurrency | rows/group | ms/group | parents/s | candidates/s | scratch bytes |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 1024 | 1 | 1024 | 61.1542 | 16744.6 | 401869.4 | 328253440 |
| 1 | 512 | 2 | 1024 | 59.7437 | 17139.9 | 411356.9 | 328253440 |

## Aggregate

| version | aggregate parents/s | aggregate candidates/s |
|---|---:|---:|
| v3 warp attention | 19520.0 | 468478.3 |
| v4 tensor GEMM attention | 33884.5 | 813226.3 |

## Comparison To PyTorch Notebook Fast Path

The public `vladkuznetsov266/transformer-inference-example` fast PyTorch path measured on Kaggle T4:

- Direct model forward best: `734786.8` candidates/s at `batch=2048`.
- Searcher-like `batch_process`: `630697.0` candidates/s for `65536` rows with `eval_batch_size=16384`.

The current native CUDA Stream1 piece-transformer backend is now about:

- `401869.4 / 734786.8 = 0.55x` of best direct PyTorch forward on GPU0.
- `411356.9 / 734786.8 = 0.56x` of best direct PyTorch forward on GPU1.
- `401869.4 / 630697.0 = 0.64x` of PyTorch `batch_process` on GPU0.
- `411356.9 / 630697.0 = 0.65x` of PyTorch `batch_process` on GPU1.

## Interpretation

This is not a hard performance ceiling. The tensor-GEMM attention rewrite moved Kaggle T4 throughput from about `232k-236k` candidates/s per T4 in v3 to about `402k-411k` candidates/s per T4 in v4, but the same checkpoint's PyTorch fast path is still `1.5x-1.8x` faster depending on comparison mode.

The remaining likely bottlenecks are not the QK/PV tensor attention itself. Local Nsight after the rewrite showed most time in transformer block linear GEMMs and unfused elementwise kernels: LayerNorm materialization, bias+SiLU, QKV bias, residual+bias add, softmax, V packing, and final quantization.

Next plausible optimization work:

1. Move FFN `bias + SiLU` into the first FFN GEMM epilogue.
2. Move attention-out and FFN2 `bias + residual add` into GEMM epilogues.
3. Write QKV GEMM output directly in the layout needed by QK/PV and apply QKV bias there, removing the separate QKV-bias and V-pack kernels.
4. Fuse the fixed `seq_len=51` softmax and PV staging more tightly, or use one persistent attention kernel if it beats CUTLASS `GemmArray` launch overhead on T4.
5. Keep MLP path separate; these are transformer-only changes.

Target: matching PyTorch `batch_process` (`~0.63M` candidates/s per T4) is realistic. Matching or slightly exceeding direct PyTorch forward (`~0.73M` candidates/s per T4) may require deeper epilogue fusion and fewer kernel launches.

## Artifacts

Downloaded output directory: `test_results/kaggle_t4_transformer_benchmark_v4_output/`

Useful files:

- `stream1_transformer_benchmark_rows.csv`
- `stream1_transformer_benchmark_logs/stream1_transformer_benchmark_gpu0.log`
- `stream1_transformer_benchmark_logs/stream1_transformer_benchmark_gpu1.log`
- `stream1_transformer_benchmark_reports/stream1_transformer_benchmark_gpu0.md`
- `stream1_transformer_benchmark_reports/stream1_transformer_benchmark_gpu1.md`
- `cayley-beam-transformer-2xt4-benchmark.log`

    log_path = BENCH_LOG_DIR / f'stream1_transformer_benchmark_gpu{gpu}.log'
    env = os.environ.copy()
    env.update({'CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES': str(gpu), 'BEAM_WEIGHT_DIR': str(WEIGHT_OUT_DIR), 'BEAM_STREAM_BENCH_REPORT': str(report_path)})
    cmd = [str(BUILD_DIR / 'stream_benchmark'), str(BENCH_PUZZLE_ID)]
    print('RUN_STREAM1_TRANSFORMER_BENCH_START', {'gpu': gpu, 'cmd': cmd, 'report': str(report_path), 'log': str(log_path)}, flush=True)
    start = time.time()
    rows = []
    with log_path.open('w', buffering=1, encoding='utf-8') as log:
        proc = subprocess.Popen(cmd, cwd=REPO_DIR, env=env, stdout=subprocess.PIPE, stderr=subprocess.STDOUT, text=True, bufsize=1)
        assert proc.stdout is not None
        for line in proc.stdout:
            log.write(line)
            print(line, end='', flush=True)
            match = row_pattern.search(line)
            if match:
                row = match.groupdict()
                row = {'gpu': gpu, 'b_micro': int(row['b_micro']), 'concurrency': int(row['concurrency']), 'rows_per_launch_group': int(row['rows']), 'ms_per_launch_group': float(row['ms']), 'parents_per_sec': float(row['parents']), 'candidates_per_sec': float(row['candidates']), 'scratch_bytes': int(row['scratch'])}
                rows.append(row)
                all_rows.append(row)
        rc = proc.wait()
    elapsed = time.time() - start
    print('RUN_STREAM1_TRANSFORMER_BENCH_DONE', {'gpu': gpu, 'rc': rc, 'seconds': elapsed, 'rows': len(rows)}, flush=True)
    if rc != 0:
        raise subprocess.CalledProcessError(rc, cmd)
    if not rows:
        raise RuntimeError(f'no stream1_transformer_micro rows parsed for gpu {gpu}')

# Work With Kaggle

Use this skill whenever the task involves Kaggle notebooks, kernels, datasets, or GPU validation runs.

## Project Memory First

For project repositories, read local project guidance before changing notebooks or source:

- `AGENTS.md`
- `memory/PROMPTS.md` and `memory/CHANGELOG.md`, especially recent Kaggle entries
- relevant `test_results/kaggle_*` reports for the same failure/config family

This project has many Kaggle-specific decisions preserved in memory. Do not assume a generic Kaggle workflow is correct when project memory says otherwise.

## Safety And Scope

- Do not upload or publish current uncommitted source to a Kaggle dataset unless the user explicitly approves external export.
- Prefer GitHub-backed Kaggle source when the user has selected GitHub as source of truth.
- Do not push a public Kaggle notebook/kernel unless the user explicitly approves publication or public push.
- Do not make C++/CUDA algorithmic changes while diagnosing Kaggle failures unless the user explicitly approves code changes. Config-only notebook changes are acceptable when useful and consistent with the request.
- Do not revert unrelated local changes. Kaggle notebook edits often coexist with dirty source changes.
- For multi-rank validation, do not fake `WORLD_SIZE=2` on one <truncated omitted_approx_tokens="707" />ed logs. This rule came from project memory and applies to diagnostic/watch tasks.
- Do not stop production/no-timeout runs unless the user explicitly asks or the notebook itself has a configured timeout.
- If a run appears hung because stdout is redirected, check rank log files and whether live-rank streaming is enabled before assuming a stall.
- Preserve logs even when stopping: terminate gracefully when possible, then download output files.

## Failure Diagnosis Checklist

After a Kaggle failure, collect and scan:

- Kaggle worker status and kernel version
- notebook stdout tail
- rank logs under the notebook output directory
- result JSONL/CSV summaries
- GPU budget/preflight output
- explicit Kaggle messages such as disk quota failures

Common patterns in this project:

- `KernelWorkerStatus.ERROR` plus "more disk space than is available" usually means disk quota/static history arena pressure.
- `return_code=-9` can indicate host RAM OOM.
- `code=3002` / `STREAM_FATAL_STREAM3_DOUBLE_BUFFER_OVERFLOW` points to Stream3/Stream4/threshold/capacity flow diagnostics.
- `final selected count does not match score phase counts` points to final selection/histogram/exact-count mismatch.
- `cudaGraphInstantiate: out of memory` may require config-only headroom/shard-capacity changes before deeper code work.
- Silent output after build may simply mean rank stdout is redirected and live rank logging is disabled.

Write a short report under `test_results/` for meaningful Kaggle validations, sweeps, failures, or launches.

## Response Style

- Be explicit about whether you changed source code, notebook config only, or only launched/monitored a run.
- Include Kaggle kernel slug/version/status when known.
- Include exact local report/log paths in `test_results/`.
- Keep recommendations tied to evidence from logs, not guesses.
- If the next step requires exporting source or publishing publicly, ask for explicit approval first.

# Project Agent Rules

## Operating Rule
- Read `AGENTS.md` before project edits.
- Update `memory/CHANGELOG.md` after meaningful code, config, or architecture changes.
- Update `memory/PROMPTS.md` with user prompts that define requirements.
- Store test outputs, logs, and verification notes under `test_results/`.
- Prefer agent-centered code: explicit contracts, deterministic tests, small files, low hidden state.

## Architecture Source
- Primary architecture contract: `ARCHITECTURE_NEED.md`.
- Current Stream 4 implementation rule: use threshold + compact + CUB/fixed-temp sort/reduce + compact; do not use Stream 4 hash-table dedup.

## Implementation Guardrails
- `State128` logical bytes are `v[0..119]`.
- `State128` padding bytes are `v[120..127]`.
- Persistent frontier padding must be zero.
- `FinalResponse` may temporarily store `target_local_idx` in `v[120..123]`.
- `Hash128` is one logical 128-bit key stored as `{lo, hi}`.
- `CandidateMeta` must remain 32 bytes and 32-byte aligned.
- Stream 3 payload id must be original candidate id, not compact index.
- Stream 4 must not apply shard top-k or semantic shard cap.

## Comparison To PyTorch Notebook Fast Path

The public `vladkuznetsov266/transformer-inference-example` fast PyTorch path measured on Kaggle T4:

- Direct model forward best: `734786.8` candidates/s at `batch=2048`.
- Searcher-like `batch_process`: `630697.0` candidates/s for `65536` rows with `eval_batch_size=16384`.

The current native CUDA Stream1 piece-transformer backend is now about:

- `401869.4 / 734786.8 = 0.55x` of best direct PyTorch forward on GPU0.
- `411356.9 / 734786.8 = 0.56x` of best direct PyTorch forward on GPU1.
- `401869.4 / 630697.0 = 0.64x` of PyTorch `batch_process` on GPU0.
- `411356.9 / 630697.0 = 0.65x` of PyTorch `batch_process` on GPU1.

## Interpretation

This is not a hard performance ceiling. The tensor-GEMM attention rewrite moved Kaggle T4 throughput from about `232k-236k` candidates/s per T4 in v3 to about `402k-411k` candidates/s per T4 in v4, but the same checkpoint's PyTorch fast path is still `1.5x-1.8x` faster depending on comparison mode.

The remaining likely bottlenecks are not the QK/PV tensor attention itself. Local Nsight after the rewrite showed most time in transformer block linear GEMMs and unfused elementwise kernels: LayerNorm materialization, bias+SiLU, QKV bias, residual+bias add, softmax, V packing, and final quantization.

Next plausible optimization work:

1. Move FFN `bias + SiLU` into the first FFN GEMM epilogue.
2. Move attention-out and FFN2 `bias + residual add` into GEMM epilogues.
3. Write QKV GEMM output directly in the layout needed by QK/PV and apply QKV bias there, removing the separate QKV-bias and V-pack kernels.
4. Fuse the fixed `seq_len=51` softmax and PV staging more tightly, or use one persistent attention kernel if it beats CUTLASS `GemmArray` launch overhead on T4.
5. Keep MLP path separate; these are transformer-only changes.

Target: matching PyTorch `batch_process` (`~0.63M` candidates/s per T4) is realistic. Matching or slightly exceeding direct PyTorch forward (`~0.73M` candidates/s per T4) may require deeper epilogue fusion and fewer kernel launches.

# Stream1 Transformer Kaggle 2xT4 Benchmark v5 - 2026-07-01

Kernel: `trydotatwo/cayley-beam-transformer-2xt4-benchmark`
Version: 5
Status: `KernelWorkerStatus.COMPLETE`
Branch commit tested: `78052c5` (`Clean Stream1 transformer tensor attention`)

The notebook now fails fast if the GitHub checkout is not commit `78052c5`.
The downloaded Kaggle log confirms:

```text
GITHUB_COMMIT= 78052c5
```

## Best Rows

| GPU | B_MICRO | concurrency | rows/group | ms/group | parents/s | candidates/s | scratch bytes |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 512 | 2 | 1024 | 60.5191 | 16920.3 | 406086.6 | 327729152 |
| 1 | 512 | 2 | 1024 | 59.2023 | 17296.6 | 415118.8 | 327729152 |

## Aggregate

| version | aggregate parents/s | aggregate candidates/s |
|---|---:|---:|
| v4 tensor GEMM attention | 33884.5 | 813226.3 |
| v5 clean tensor attention | 34216.9 | 821205.4 |

v5 is only `1.01x` v4 on Kaggle 2xT4. The local clean-path improvement did not
transfer to Kaggle T4 throughput.

## Comparison To PyTorch Notebook Fast Path

The public `vladkuznetsov266/transformer-inference-example` fast PyTorch path
measured on Kaggle T4:

- Direct model forward best: `734786.8` candidates/s at `batch=2048`.
- Searcher-like `batch_process`: `630697.0` candidates/s for `65536` rows with
  `eval_batch_size=16384`.

Per T4, the current native CUDA Stream1 piece-transformer backend is:

- GPU0: `406086.6 / 734786.8 = 0.55x` of direct PyTorch forward.
- GPU1: `415118.8 / 734786.8 = 0.57x` of direct PyTorch forward.
- GPU0: `406086.6 / 630697.0 = 0.64x` of PyTorch `batch_process`.
- GPU1: `415118.8 / 630697.0 = 0.66x` of PyTorch `batch_process`.

So yes: on Kaggle T4, the native backend is still slower than the PyTorch fast
path per GPU, despite running correctly and using the intended commit.

## Interpretation

This run proves the clean tensor attention rewrite did not fix the Kaggle T4
performance gap. The best point stayed at `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=2` on both
T4s, with roughly the same scratch footprint as v4.

The next optimization should not assume the local Docker microbenchmark result
represents Kaggle T4. The Kaggle evidence points back to profiling the exact T4
runtime and reducing transformer-only launch/elementwise overhead around the
linear GEMMs, LayerNorms, residual adds, bias+activation, and QKV/V layout path.

## Artifacts

Downloaded output directory:
`test_results/kaggle_t4_transformer_benchmark_v5_output/`

Useful files:

- `stream1_transformer_benchmark_rows.csv`
- `cayley-beam-transformer-2xt4-benchmark.log`

- `stream1_transformer_benchmark_reports/stream1_transformer_benchmark_gpu1.md`

# Stream1 Transformer Kaggle 2xT4 Benchmark v5 - 2026-07-01

Kernel: `trydotatwo/cayley-beam-transformer-2xt4-benchmark`
Version: 5
Status: `KernelWorkerStatus.COMPLETE`
Branch commit tested: `78052c5` (`Clean Stream1 transformer tensor attention`)

The notebook now fails fast if the GitHub checkout is not commit `78052c5`.
The downloaded Kaggle log confirms:

```text
GITHUB_COMMIT= 78052c5
```

## Best Rows

| GPU | B_MICRO | concurrency | rows/group | ms/group | parents/s | candidates/s | scratch bytes |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 512 | 2 | 1024 | 60.5191 | 16920.3 | 406086.6 | 327729152 |
| 1 | 512 | 2 | 1024 | 59.2023 | 17296.6 | 415118.8 | 327729152 |

## Aggregate

| version | aggregate parents/s | aggregate candidates/s |
|---|---:|---:|
| v4 tensor GEMM attention | 33884.5 | 813226.3 |
| v5 clean tensor attention | 34216.9 | 821205.4 |

v5 is only `1.01x` v4 on Kaggle 2xT4. The local clean-path improvement did not
transfer to Kaggle T4 throughput.

## Comparison To PyTorch Notebook Fast Path

The public `vladkuznetsov266/transformer-inference-example` fast PyTorch path
measured on Kaggle T4:

- Direct model forward best: `734786.8` candidates/s at `batch=2048`.
- Searcher-like `batch_process`: `630697.0` candidates/s for `65536` rows with
  `eval_batch_size=16384`.

Per T4, the current native CUDA Stream1 piece-transformer backend is:

- GPU0: `406086.6 / 734786.8 = 0.55x` of direct PyTorch forward.
- GPU1: `415118.8 / 734786.8 = 0.57x` of direct PyTorch forward.
- GPU0: `406086.6 / 630697.0 = 0.64x` of PyTorch `batch_process`.
- GPU1: `415118.8 / 630697.0 = 0.66x` of PyTorch `batch_process`.

So yes: on Kaggle T4, the native backend is still slower than the PyTorch fast
path per GPU, despite running correctly and using the intended commit.

## Interpretation

This run proves the clean tensor attention rewrite did not fix the Kaggle T4
performance gap. The best point stayed at `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=2` on both
T4s, with roughly the same scratch footprint as v4.

The next optimization should not assume the local Docker microbenchmark result
represents Kaggle T4. The Kaggle evidence points back to profiling the exact T4
runtime and reducing transformer-only launch/elementwise overhead around the
linear GEMMs, LayerNorms, residual adds, bias+activation, and QKV/V layout path.

## Artifacts

Downloaded output directory:
`test_results/kaggle_t4_transformer_benchmark_v5_output/`

Useful files:

- `stream1_transformer_benchmark_rows.csv`
- `cayley-beam-transformer-2xt4-benchmark.log`

- `stream1_transformer_benchmark_reports/stream1_transformer_benchmark_gpu0.md`
- `stream1_transformer_benchmark_reports/stream1_transformer_benchmark_gpu1.md`

inline std::uint64_t stream1_scratch_bytes(
    const Stream1ModelConfig& model,
    std::uint32_t b_micro,
    std::uint32_t lane_count) {
    const std::uint64_t rows = stream1_inference_rows(b_micro, model) * lane_count;
    switch (model.backend) {
    case STREAM1_BACKEND_MLP:
        return fp16_bytes(rows * (static_cast<std::uint64_t>(model.hidden1) + 2ULL * model.hidden2 + model.output_dim));
    case STREAM1_BACKEND_PIECE_TRANSFORMER:
        return transformer_scratch_bytes_for_rows(model, rows);
    default:
        throw std::runtime_error("unsupported Stream1 backend in scratch byte helper");
    }
}

inline std::uint64_t total_device_weight_bytes(const HostWeightBytes& weights) {
    return total_host_weight_bytes(weights);
}

#ifndef BEAM_STREAM1_WEIGHT_IO_MANIFEST_ONLY
inline const half* weight_half_data(const std::vector<std::byte>& bytes) {
    return reinterpret_cast<const half*>(bytes.data());
}

inline void copy_bytes_to_device(void* dst, const std::vector<std::byte>& bytes, const char* name) {
    if (dst == nullptr || bytes.empty()) {
        throw std::runtime_error(std::string("invalid device copy input: ") + name);
    }
    BEAM_CUDA_CHECK(cudaMemcpy(dst, bytes.data(), bytes.size(), cudaMemcpyHostToDevice));
}

inline void require_aligned(const void* ptr, std::uintptr_t alignment, const char* name) {
    if (reinterpret_cast<std::uintptr_t>(ptr) % alignment != 0) {
        throw std::runtime_error(std::string("device pointer alignment failed: ") + name);
    }
}

template <typename T>
inline void alloc_and_copy_typed(T*& dst, const std::vector<std::byte>& src, const char* name) {
    BEAM_CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(reinterpret_cast<void**>(&dst), src.size()));
    copy_bytes_to_device(dst, src, name);
    require_aligned(dst, 16, name);
}

inline void free_stream1_scratch(ScratchAllocation& scratch) {
    cudaFree(scratch.hidden1);
    cudaFree(scratch.hidden2);
    cudaFree(scratch.residual);
    cudaFree(scratch.output);
    cudaFree(scratch.transformer_tokens);
    cudaFree(scratch.transformer_qkv);
    cudaFree(scratch.transformer_attention_scores_probs);
    cudaFree(scratch.transformer_attention_context);
    cudaFree(scratch.transformer_ff_hidden);
    cudaFree(scratch.transformer_logits);
    scratch = ScratchAllocation{};
}

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Benchmark exported Stream1 MLP weights with PyTorch.

This is a diagnostic benchmark for the exported runtime weight directory. It
uses the same folded position-class input table as the CUDA Stream1 MLP path and
reports candidate throughput as rows * MOVE_COUNT / ms.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import json
from pathlib import Path
import re
import time
from typing import Dict, Iterable, List

import numpy as np
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F

MOVE_COUNT = 24
SCORE_MAX_Q = 300.0
SCORE_SCALE = 1024.0
SCORE_MAX_KEY = int(SCORE_MAX_Q * SCORE_SCALE)


def load_manifest(weight_dir: Path) -> Dict[str, object]:
    with (weight_dir / "manifest.json").open("r", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        return json.load(fh)


def suffix_for(manifest: Dict[str, object]) -> str:
    dtype = str(manifest.get("dtype", "fp16"))
    if dtype != "fp16":
        raise ValueError(f"torch MLP benchmark currently expects fp16 weights, got dtype={dtype}")
    return ".fp16"


def load_half(path: Path, shape: Iterable[int], device: torch.device) -> torch.Tensor:
    shape_tuple = tuple(int(x) for x in shape)
    arr = np.fromfile(path, dtype=np.float16)
    expected = int(np.prod(shape_tuple))
    if arr.size != expected:
        raise ValueError(f"{path} size mismatch: got {arr.size}, expected {expected} for shape={shape_tuple}")
    return torch.from_numpy(arr.reshape(shape_tuple)).to(device=device, dtype=torch.float16)


def parse_initial_state(test_csv: Path, puzzle_id: int, state_len: int) -> List[int]:
    with test_csv.open("r", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        reader = csv.DictReader(fh)
        for row in reader:
            raw_id = row.get("id") or row.get("puzzle_id") or row.get("ID")
            if raw_id is None or int(raw_id) != puzzle_id:
                continue
            raw_state = row.get("initial_state") or row.get("state")
            if raw_state is None:
                values = list(row.values())
                raw_state = values[1] if len(values) > 1 else ""
            nums = [int(x) for x in re.findall(r"\d+", raw_state)]
            if len(nums) < state_len:
                raise ValueError(f"puzzle_id={puzzle_id} has only {len(nums)} state values, expected {state_len}")
            return nums[:state_len]
    raise ValueError(f"puzzle_id={puzzle_id} not found in {test_csv}")


class ExportedStream1Mlp:
    def __init__(self, weight_dir: Path, device: torch.device) -> None:
        self.weight_dir = weight_dir
        self.device = device
        self.manifest = load_manifest(weight_dir)
        if str(self.manifest.get("backend", "mlp")) not in ("mlp", ""):
            raise ValueError(f"expected MLP backend manifest, got {self.manifest.get('backend')}")
        normalization = str(self.manifest.get("normalization", "batchnorm_folded"))
        if normalization not in ("batchnorm_folded", "", "none"):
            raise ValueError(f"this benchmark is for folded/no-normalization MLP weights, got normalization={normalization}")
        suffix = suffix_for(self.manifest)
        self.state_len = int(self.manifest["state_len"])
        self.num_classes = int(self.manifest["num_classes"])

inline std::uint64_t stream1_scratch_bytes(
    const Stream1ModelConfig& model,
    std::uint32_t b_micro,
    std::uint32_t lane_count) {
    const std::uint64_t rows = stream1_inference_rows(b_micro, model) * lane_count;
    switch (model.backend) {
    case STREAM1_BACKEND_MLP:
        return fp16_bytes(rows * (static_cast<std::uint64_t>(model.hidden1) + 2ULL * model.hidden2 + model.output_dim));
    case STREAM1_BACKEND_PIECE_TRANSFORMER:
        return transformer_scratch_bytes_for_rows(model, rows);
    default:
        throw std::runtime_error("unsupported Stream1 backend in scratch byte helper");
    }
}

inline std::uint64_t total_device_weight_bytes(const HostWeightBytes& weights) {
    return total_host_weight_bytes(weights);
}

#ifndef BEAM_STREAM1_WEIGHT_IO_MANIFEST_ONLY
inline const half* weight_half_data(const std::vector<std::byte>& bytes) {
    return reinterpret_cast<const half*>(bytes.data());

inline void copy_bytes_to_device(void* dst, const std::vector<std::byte>& bytes, const char* name) {
    if (dst == nullptr || bytes.empty()) {
        throw std::runtime_error(std::string("invalid device copy input: ") + name);
    }
    BEAM_CUDA_CHECK(cudaMemcpy(dst, bytes.data(), bytes.size(), cudaMemcpyHostToDevice));
}

inline void require_aligned(const void* ptr, std::uintptr_t alignment, const char* name) {
    if (reinterpret_cast<std::uintptr_t>(ptr) % alignment != 0) {
        throw std::runtime_error(std::string("device pointer alignment failed: ") + name);
    }
}

template <typename T>
inline void alloc_and_copy_typed(T*& dst, const std::vector<std::byte>& src, const char* name) {
    BEAM_CUDA_CHECK(cudaMalloc(reinterpret_cast<void**>(&dst), src.size()));
    copy_bytes_to_device(dst, src, name);
    require_aligned(dst, 16, name);
}

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Benchmark exported Stream1 MLP weights with PyTorch.

This is a diagnostic benchmark for the exported runtime weight directory. It
uses the same folded position-class input table as the CUDA Stream1 MLP path and
reports candidate throughput as rows * MOVE_COUNT / ms.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import argparse
import csv
import json
from pathlib import Path
import re
import time
from typing import Dict, Iterable, List

import numpy as np
import torch
import torch.nn.functional as F

MOVE_COUNT = 24
SCORE_MAX_Q = 300.0
SCORE_SCALE = 1024.0
SCORE_MAX_KEY = int(SCORE_MAX_Q * SCORE_SCALE)


def load_manifest(weight_dir: Path) -> Dict[str, object]:
    with (weight_dir / "manifest.json").open("r", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        return json.load(fh)


def suffix_for(manifest: Dict[str, object]) -> str:
    dtype = str(manifest.get("dtype", "fp16"))
    if dtype != "fp16":
        raise ValueError(f"torch MLP benchmark currently expects fp16 weights, got dtype={dtype}")
    return ".fp16"


def load_half(path: Path, shape: Iterable[int], device: torch.device) -> torch.Tensor:
    shape_tuple = tuple(int(x) for x in shape)
    arr = np.fromfile(path, dtype=np.float16)
    expected = int(np.prod(shape_tuple))
    if arr.size != expected:
        raise ValueError(f"{path} size mismatch: got {arr.size}, expected {expected} for shape={shape_tuple}")
    return torch.from_numpy(arr.reshape(shape_tuple)).to(device=device, dtype=torch.float16)


def parse_initial_state(test_csv: Path, puzzle_id: int, state_len: int) -> List[int]:
    with test_csv.open("r", encoding="utf-8", newline="") as fh:
        reader = csv.DictReader(fh)
        for row in reader:
            raw_id = row.get("id") or row.get("puzzle_id") or row.get("ID")
            if raw_id is None or int(raw_id) != puzzle_id:
                continue
            raw_state = row.get("initial_state") or row.get("state")
            if raw_state is None:
                values = list(row.values())
                raw_state = values[1] if len(values) > 1 else ""
            nums = [int(x) for x in re.findall(r"\d+", raw_state)]
            if len(nums) < state_len:
                raise ValueError(f"puzzle_id={puzzle_id} has only {len(nums)} state values, expected {state_len}")
            return nums[:state_len]
    raise ValueError(f"puzzle_id={puzzle_id} not found in {test_csv}")


class ExportedStream1Mlp:
    def __init__(self, weight_dir: Path, device: torch.device) -> None:
        self.weight_dir = weight_dir
        self.device = device
        self.manifest = load_manifest(weight_dir)
        if str(self.manifest.get("backend", "mlp")) not in ("mlp", ""):
            raise ValueError(f"expected MLP backend manifest, got {self.manifest.get('backend')}")
        normalization = str(self.manifest.get("normalization", "batchnorm_folded"))
        if normalization not in ("batchnorm_folded", "", "none"):
            raise ValueError(f"this benchmark is for folded/no-normalization MLP weights, got normalization={normalization}")
        suffix = suffix_for(self.manifest)
        self.state_len = int(self.manifest["state_len"])
        self.num_classes = int(self.manifest["num_classes"])

  builds one stable `production_runner` under `prebuilt-a100-ihes`, and
  `ihes_solution_repair.sh` can reuse it through `BEAM_PREBUILT_RUNNER` instead
  of running CMake/NVCC inside every solution-repair array task.
- Added an explicit IHES solution repair execution mode switch:
  `BEAM_SOLUTION_REPAIR_MODE=plan` runs all segments through one outer
  `torchrun`, while `BEAM_SOLUTION_REPAIR_MODE=legacy` preserves the previous
  one-`torchrun`-per-segment behavior. Unknown modes fail immediately; there is
  no automatic fallback.
- Added `run_solution_repair_plan.sh` and common `beam_torchrun_segment_plan`
  orchestration so plan mode runs the whole segment list inside one outer
  distributed launch. Both plan and legacy modes now set
  `BEAM_HISTORY_DISK_PATH` per segment so history directories remain isolated;
  plan mode leaves cleanup to the existing job-level safe cleanup after the
  outer distributed run exits.
- Fixed plan-mode launcher execution by invoking `run_solution_repair_plan.sh`
  through `/bin/bash` inside the distributed wrapper, so it does not depend on
  the repository file executable bit after `git fetch/reset`.
- Added `hpc/ihes_cube_model/prepare_solution_repair_single_window.sh` for the
  current IHES repair workflow. It prepares `solution_repair_*` CSV files with
  exactly one block window, `window = K1 + 7` by default, instead of generating
  the previous diversity sweep over `K1+1..K1+7`.
- Replaced the plan-mode repair launcher internals with a native lightweight
  rank launcher while leaving legacy mode on `torchrun`. Plan mode now starts
  `WORLD_SIZE_EFFECTIVE` shell ranks directly with `RANK`, `LOCAL_RANK`, and
  `WORLD_SIZE` set, writes only rank 0 to the main log, and stores nonzero ranks
  under the existing rank log directory.
- Added an explicit `BEAM_SOLUTION_REPAIR_MODE=legacy_na<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1830" />tation overhead rather than explained away as intrinsic model cost. Updated transformer benchmark reports accordingly; no CUDA/runtime behavior changed.
- Rewrote the Stream1 piece-transformer attention hot path to use CUTLASS tensor-core QK/PV attention instead of the scalar/warp attention kernel. The current execution path uses device pointer-array scratch plus `GemmArray` QK/PV launches, padded half score/prob/V scratch, and chunked attention batches so `B_MICRO=8192` no longer fails at `8192*8=65536` attention matrices. Local Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`; best local T4 sweep point is `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=1` at `583142.3` candidates/s. Nsight now shows the remaining bottleneck is mostly linear GEMMs and unfused elementwise transformer kernels rather than the old attention kernel. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_tensor_attention_rewrite_2026-06-30.md`.
- Rewrote the Stream1 piece-transformer attention path into a clean tensor-core implementation without pointer-array scratch or CUTLASS `GemmArray`. The new path keeps QKV/FF/output GEMMs unchanged, applies QKV bias in-place, uses per-head CUTLASS `GemmBatched` QK/PV with one padded score/prob/V scratch, and preserves the MLP Stream1 path. Local Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`; local fp16 benchmark points reached `577909.4` candidates/s at `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=1`, `592946.4` at `1024x1`, and `602873.7` at `512x2`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_clean_tensor_attention_2026-07-01.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 benchmark version 5 against commit `78052c5` with an explicit notebook commit check. Kaggle completed successfully, but clean tensor attention only reached `821205.4` aggregate candidates/s (`406086.6` on GPU0, `415118.8` on GPU1), about `1.01x` v4 and still only `0.64-0.66x` of the PyTorch `batch_process` fast path per T4. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_kaggle_benchmark_v5_2026-07-01.md`.

skip_parts = ("_original_", "_reflected_", "_sources_", "_plan_")
files = [
    p for p in sorted(logs.glob("solve_bucket_fresh_*.tsv"))
    if not any(x in p.name for x in skip_parts) and p.stat().st_size > 200
]

fields = [
    "puzzle_id", "variant", "source_index", "known_length", "found_length",
    "delta", "run_puzzle_id", "depth_index", "found_depth", "owner_rank",
    "solution_path", "source_solution",
]

by_puzzle = {}
sources = {}

for p in files:
    with p.open(newline="", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
        reader = csv.DictReader(fh, delimiter="\t")
        for row in reader:
            pid = row.get("puzzle_id", "").strip()
            sol = row.get("solution_path", "").strip()
            if not pid or not sol:
                continue
            clean = {k: row.get(k, "") for k in fields}
            key = (
                clean["puzzle_id"], clean["variant"], clean["source_index"],
                clean["found_length"], clean["solution_path"], clean["source_solution"],
            )
            by_puzzle.setdefault(pid, {})
            by_puzzle[pid][key] = clean

  builds one stable `production_runner` under `prebuilt-a100-ihes`, and
  `ihes_solution_repair.sh` can reuse it through `BEAM_PREBUILT_RUNNER` instead
  of running CMake/NVCC inside every solution-repair array task.
- Added an explicit IHES solution repair execution mode switch:
  `BEAM_SOLUTION_REPAIR_MODE=plan` runs all segments through one outer
  `torchrun`, while `BEAM_SOLUTION_REPAIR_MODE=legacy` preserves the previous
  one-`torchrun`-per-segment behavior. Unknown modes fail immediately; there is
  no automatic fallback.
- Added `run_solution_repair_plan.sh` and common `beam_torchrun_segment_plan`
  orchestration so plan mode runs the whole segment list inside one outer
  distributed launch. Both plan and legacy modes now set
  `BEAM_HISTORY_DISK_PATH` per segment so history directories remain isolated;
  plan mode leaves cleanup to the existing job-level safe cleanup after the
  outer distributed run exits.
- Fixed plan-mode launcher execution by invoking `run_solution_repair_plan.sh`
  through `/bin/bash` inside the distributed wrapper, so it does not depend on
  the repository file executable bit after `git fetch/reset`.
- Added `hpc/ihes_cube_model/prepare_solution_repair_single_window.sh` for the
  current IHES repair workflow. It prepares `solution_repair_*` CSV files with
  exactly one block window, `window = K1 + 7` by default, instead of generating
  the previous diversity sweep over `K1+1..K1+7`.
- Replaced the plan-mode repair launcher internals with a native lightweight
  rank launcher while leaving legacy mode on `torchrun`. Plan mode now starts
  `WORLD_SIZE_EFFECTIVE` shell ranks directly with `RANK`, `LOCAL_RANK`, and
  `WORLD_SIZE` set, writes only rank 0 to the main log, and stores nonzero ranks
  under the existing rank log directory.
- Added an explicit `BEAM_SOLUTION_REPAIR_MODE=legacy_na<truncated omitted_approx_tokens="1830" />tation overhead rather than explained away as intrinsic model cost. Updated transformer benchmark reports accordingly; no CUDA/runtime behavior changed.
- Rewrote the Stream1 piece-transformer attention hot path to use CUTLASS tensor-core QK/PV attention instead of the scalar/warp attention kernel. The current execution path uses device pointer-array scratch plus `GemmArray` QK/PV launches, padded half score/prob/V scratch, and chunked attention batches so `B_MICRO=8192` no longer fails at `8192*8=65536` attention matrices. Local Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`; best local T4 sweep point is `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=1` at `583142.3` candidates/s. Nsight now shows the remaining bottleneck is mostly linear GEMMs and unfused elementwise transformer kernels rather than the old attention kernel. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_tensor_attention_rewrite_2026-06-30.md`.
- Rewrote the Stream1 piece-transformer attention path into a clean tensor-core implementation without pointer-array scratch or CUTLASS `GemmArray`. The new path keeps QKV/FF/output GEMMs unchanged, applies QKV bias in-place, uses per-head CUTLASS `GemmBatched` QK/PV with one padded score/prob/V scratch, and preserves the MLP Stream1 path. Local Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`; local fp16 benchmark points reached `577909.4` candidates/s at `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=1`, `592946.4` at `1024x1`, and `602873.7` at `512x2`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_clean_tensor_attention_2026-07-01.md`.
- Ran Kaggle 2xT4 benchmark version 5 against commit `78052c5` with an explicit notebook commit check. Kaggle completed successfully, but clean tensor attention only reached `821205.4` aggregate candidates/s (`406086.6` on GPU0, `415118.8` on GPU1), about `1.01x` v4 and still only `0.64-0.66x` of the PyTorch `batch_process` fast path per T4. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_kaggle_benchmark_v5_2026-07-01.md`.

 - Rewrote the Stream1 piece-transformer attention path into a clean tensor-core implementation without pointer-array scratch or CUTLASS `GemmArray`. The new path keeps QKV/FF/output GEMMs unchanged, applies QKV bias in-place, uses per-head CUTLASS `GemmBatched` QK/PV with one padded score/prob/V scratch, and preserves the MLP Stream1 path. Local Docker verification passed `stream1_transformer_cuda_tests`; local fp16 benchmark points reached `577909.4` candidates/s at `B_MICRO=512, concurrency=1`, `592946.4` at `1024x1`, and `602873.7` at `512x2`. Evidence: `test_results/stream1_transformer_clean_tensor_attention_2026-07-01.md`.
 - Ran Kaggle 2xT4 benchmark version 5 against commit `78052c5` with an explicit notebook commit check. Kaggle completed successfully, but clean tensor attention only reached `821205.4` aggregate candidates/s (`406086.6` on GPU0, `415118.8` on GPU1), about `1.01x` v4 and still only `0.64-0.66x` of the PyTorch `batch_process` fast path per T4. # Stream1 MLP Native vs PyTorch Kaggle 2xT4 - 2026-07-01

Kaggle kernel: `trydotatwo/cayley-beam-mlp-native-vs-torch-2xt4-benchmark`, version 2.

Source tag: `stream1-mlp-torch-kaggle-v2-code` at `cc90a3d`.

Status: `KernelWorkerStatus.COMPLETE`.

## Best Per-GPU Throughput

| GPU | Native B_MICRO | Native concurrency | Native candidates/s | Torch rows | Torch candidates/s | Native / Torch |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 0 | 4096 | 1 | 16,416,335.8 | 16,384 | 8,133,621.3 | 2.0183x |
| 1 | 4096 | 1 | 15,728,371.2 | 4,096 | 6,763,818.6 | 2.3254x |

Aggregate best throughput across both T4 GPUs:

- Native CUTLASS MLP: 32,144,707.0 candidates/s.
- PyTorch preindexed MLP: 14,897,439.9 candidates/s.
- Native / Torch aggregate: 2.1577x.

## Interpretation

For the exported folded MLP weights, the native CUTLASS Stream1 path is about 2.0-2.3x faster than a direct PyTorch implementation on Kaggle T4. This establishes the local comparison baseline for judging the transformer backend: the transformer PyTorch fast path being faster than native means the transformer native path still has implementation overhead, not that native CUDA is inherently slower than Torch for this project.
